FILE: README.md

<div align="center">

# Test-Time Compute for Tabular Foundation Models

**Mechanisms, Gains, and Limits**

[![arXiv](https://img.shields.io/badge/arXiv-2610.12005-b31b1b.svg)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.12005)
[![Results](https://img.shields.io/badge/%F0%9F%A4%97%20Results-Hugging%20Face-yellow)](https://huggingface.co/datasets/nkh/test-time-compute-for-tabular-foundation-models-results)
[![License](https://img.shields.io/badge/License-Apache%202.0-blue.svg)](LICENSE)
[![Python](https://img.shields.io/badge/python-3.11-blue.svg)](envs/environment.yml)

</div>

<p align="center">
  <img src="docs/assets/framework.png" width="100%" alt="Three families of test-time compute act on different parts of the inference path of a tabular foundation model.">
</p>

Which forms of test-time compute improve the predictions of strong pretrained tabular foundation
models (TFMs)? We study three axes on **TabPFN-3**, **TabICL v2** and **TabFM**, evaluated on all
51 datasets of [TabArena](https://github.com/autogluon/tabarena) and on large OpenML tables:

- **Adaptation** changes the model parameters: full finetuning and **DiagScale**, a
  zero-initialized diagonal multiplier on the query–key similarity.
- **Aggregation** changes the prediction pool: a pool of TabPFN-3 preprocessing/inference
  configurations, combined by out-of-fold greedy ensemble selection.
- **Context construction** changes the conditioning data: attention-guided row retrieval, which
  also scores source pools larger than fit in GPU memory.

This repository contains the evaluation protocol, these methods and the configs of the paper's
main experiments.

## Key findings

- **DiagScale matches full finetuning with 0.003–0.03% of the parameters** on all three
  backbones.
- **Aggregation needs both a diverse pool and a selective reducer.** More native views saturate
  quickly; over 96 configurations, greedy selection lowers error by 2.4%, whereas uniform
  averaging of the same predictions increases it.
- **Context construction helps in some regimes.** Attention-guided retrieval improves TabPFN-3 on
  some large tables and extends it to source pools of up to 10M rows; the context-expansion methods
  we tested give no consistent gain.
- **Adaptation and aggregation combine** on TabPFN-3, at about 40x the compute of DiagScale
  alone.

## Main results

TabArena Elo (see [Evaluation](#evaluation)); ΔElo is against the TabArena default of the same
backbone (for TabFM, our frozen run). GPU-h: total over the 816 cells on one H100.

| Backbone | Method | Elo | ΔElo | GPU-h |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|
| TabPFN-3 | frozen (8 views) | 1658 | +1 | 0.5 |
| | 32 native views | 1675 | +21 | 1.7 |
| | full finetuning | 1681 | +25 | 7.7 |
| | **DiagScale** | 1682 | +26 | 6.6 |
| | 96 configurations + greedy selection | 1721 | +67 | 247.5 |
| | **DiagScale + aggregation** | **1748** | **+93** | 253.1 |
| TabICL v2 | frozen | 1592 | +9 | 1.2 |
| | full finetuning | 1659 | +77 | 10.9 |
| | **DiagScale** | 1664 | +82 | 10.8 |
| TabFM | frozen | 1792 | +0 | 19.3 |
| | full finetuning | 1820 | +28 | 61.9 |
| | **DiagScale** | 1812 | +20 | 59.4 |

## Installation

Requires Python 3.11 and a CUDA GPU (the paper used one 80 GB H100 per run).

```bash
git clone https://github.com/kanghui-learning/test-time-compute-for-tabular-foundation-models.git
cd test-time-compute-for-tabular-foundation-models
conda env create -f envs/environment.yml   # creates the env "tfm-ttc"
conda activate tfm-ttc
bash scripts/install_forks.sh              # patched tabpfn / tabicl / tabfm, tabarena @ abd24c7
pip install -e .
```

The installer clones each backbone at a fixed upstream commit, applies [`patches/`](patches/README.md)
(finetuning hooks, DiagScale, bf16 TabFM inference) and checks the result against the paper's code.

<details>
<summary><b>Model weights, data and caches</b></summary>

Weights are downloaded by each backbone on first use.

| Backbone | Weights | License |
|---|---|---|
| TabPFN-3 | Prior Labs, downloaded by `tabpfn`: accept the license at [ux.priorlabs.ai](https://ux.priorlabs.ai), then `export TABPFN_TOKEN=<API key>` (`TABPFN_MODEL_CACHE_DIR` sets the cache) | TabPFN-3 model license (non-commercial) |
| TabICL v2 | [`jingang/TabICL`](https://huggingface.co/jingang/TabICL), downloaded by `tabicl` | BSD-3-Clause |
| TabFM | [`google/tabfm-1.0.0-pytorch`](https://huggingface.co/google/tabfm-1.0.0-pytorch): accept the license on the model page, then `hf auth login` | TabFM Non-Commercial License v1.0 |

TabArena datasets and splits are downloaded from OpenML on first use (`OPENML_CACHE_DIR` sets the
cache). All outputs go to `$TTC_RUNS_DIR` (default `./runs`); fit caches can be large, so point it
at a data disk.

The paper environment used AutoGluon nightly `1.5.1b20260702`; `envs/environment.yml` pins the
closest stable release (1.5.0), which reproduced the frozen results bit for bit in our checks.

</details>

## Quick start

Run DiagScale on TabPFN-3 on two small TabArena datasets (diabetes and airfoil_self_noise, one
fold); it prints a TabArena leaderboard for these two datasets:

```bash
python -m ttc.run configs/smoke/tabpfn3_diagscale.yaml
```

Every config in `configs/` has a quick version in `configs/smoke/`. Elo is defined on the full
816-cell protocol; to reproduce a paper Elo from the released results:

```bash
hf download nkh/test-time-compute-for-tabular-foundation-models-results \
    --repo-type dataset --local-dir hf_results
python -m ttc.eval.elo \
    --candidate hf_results/raw/tabpfn3_diagscale/results_per_split.csv \
    --reference-field hf_results/raw/tabfm_frozen/results_per_split.csv \
    --baseline "TA-TABPFN-3 (default)"            # ΔElo +26
```

## Reproducing the main experiments

```bash
python -m ttc.run configs/<backbone>/<method>.yaml
```

Each config runs one method on the full TabArena protocol (51 datasets, 816 dataset × repeat ×
fold cells) and writes `runs/eval/<tag>/results_per_split.csv`. Finished cells are cached, so a
long run can be split across jobs with a `datasets:` list in the config.

| Config | Method |
|---|---|
| [`tabpfn3/frozen.yaml`](configs/tabpfn3/frozen.yaml) | frozen TabPFN-3, 8 views |
| [`tabpfn3/full_ft.yaml`](configs/tabpfn3/full_ft.yaml) | full finetuning, lr 1e-6 |
| [`tabpfn3/diagscale.yaml`](configs/tabpfn3/diagscale.yaml) | DiagScale, lr 3e-2 |
| [`tabpfn3/native_views_{8,16,32,64}.yaml`](configs/tabpfn3) | V native views |
| [`tabpfn3/aggregation_96.yaml`](configs/tabpfn3/aggregation_96.yaml) | 96 configurations + greedy selection |
| [`tabpfn3/diagscale_aggregation_96.yaml`](configs/tabpfn3/diagscale_aggregation_96.yaml) | DiagScale, then the 96-configuration pool |
| [`tabiclv2/{frozen,full_ft,diagscale}.yaml`](configs/tabiclv2) | TabICL v2: frozen / full FT, lr 1e-5 / DiagScale, lr 3e-2 |
| [`tabfm/frozen.yaml`](configs/tabfm/frozen.yaml) | frozen TabFM, bf16, 32 views; the 68th Elo reference |
| [`tabfm/{full_ft,diagscale}.yaml`](configs/tabfm) | TabFM: full FT, lr 1e-4 / DiagScale, lr 1e-2 |

Adaptation: at most 30 epochs, patience 8, AdamW weight decay 0.01, one learning rate per backbone
and method; 2 views for training and validation, the backbone's default views at deployment (8, or
32 for TabFM). The pretrained weights are kept when no epoch improves validation.

Aggregation: the pool is fixed by its seed; [`configs/aggregation_pool/`](configs/aggregation_pool)
lists the 96 configurations (the first K form the K-configuration pool), and
`tests/test_aggregation_pool.py` checks that the sampler reproduces them.

### Row retrieval on large tables

```bash
python -m ttc.benchmarks.row_pool_prep --datasets Higgs
python -m ttc.benchmarks.row_modelaware --datasets Higgs --n-avail 10000000 --seeds 0 \
    --arms full modelaware --overflow split
```

Top-500 attention retrieval per query, queries clustered by their retrieved sets; pools above
about 4M rows are scored in 2M-row blocks, and oversized unions are truncated or split
(`--overflow`). Datasets, data preparation and the paper's grid:
[`docs/retrieval.md`](docs/retrieval.md).

## Evaluation

```bash
python -m ttc.eval.paired --candidate <a.csv> --baseline <b.csv>
```

- **Elo** (`ttc.eval.elo`, as in [Quick start](#quick-start)): TabArena's Bradley–Terry fit against
  the fixed 68-entry reference field, one candidate per fit, calibrated to RandomForest (default) =
  1000. The 67 TabArena references are fetched from TabArena; frozen TabFM comes from
  `--reference-field`.
- **Paired error change:** log error ratios averaged within each dataset, then over datasets, with
  a 95% dataset-bootstrap interval.

See [`docs/evaluation.md`](docs/evaluation.md).

## Repository structure

```
configs/            experiment configs (one per paper method), smoke versions, the 96-configuration pool
src/ttc/
  run.py            YAML config -> one TabArena run
  models.py         full finetuning / DiagScale for TabPFN-3, TabICL v2 and TabFM
  agg_model.py      TabPFN-3 configuration pool + greedy reducer
  agg_ft_model.py   DiagScale followed by the configuration pool
  aggregation/      configuration search space and reducers
  backbones/        TabFM wrapper; TabICL and TabFM finetuning loops
  benchmarks/       TabArena runner; large-table row retrieval
  eval/             Elo and paired error changes
patches/            patches to tabpfn, tabicl and tabfm, with their licenses
scripts/            fork installer, export of results for Hugging Face
docs/               evaluation and retrieval details
tests/              unit tests (pool reproducibility, Elo, retrieval split)
```

<details>
<summary><b>Reproducibility notes</b></summary>

- Frozen inference and every aggregation member reproduced the paper runs bit for bit.
- GPU finetuning is not bitwise deterministic: two runs of the same config differed by up to 0.9%
  in error on a cell.

</details>

## Acknowledgements

This work builds on [TabArena](https://github.com/autogluon/tabarena) for the benchmark and
evaluation, and on the [TabPFN](https://github.com/PriorLabs/TabPFN),
[TabICL](https://github.com/soda-inria/tabicl) and [TabFM](https://github.com/google-research/tabfm)
backbones. The row-retrieval pipeline adapts the retrieval of
[LimiX](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.03505) to TabPFN-3.

Built with PriorLabs-TabPFN.

FILE: THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md

# Third-party notices

This repository does not vendor third-party source code. It ships **patches** (in `patches/`)
against the projects below, plus a script (`scripts/install_forks.sh`) that downloads the
unmodified upstream sources, applies the patches locally and installs them. No model weights
(pretrained or fine-tuned) are distributed with this repository; each library downloads its
own weights from its publisher under the publisher's terms.

The patch files are derivative works of the respective upstream projects and are distributed
under those projects' licenses, listed below.

---

## TabPFN (Prior Labs)

- Upstream: https://github.com/PriorLabs/TabPFN (base: tag v8.0.7, commit `95ff6772`)
- Patch: `patches/tabpfn/`, `patches/tabpfn.diff` (modified files under `src/tabpfn/finetuning/`)
- Code license: **Prior Labs License v1.2 (Apache 2.0 with ADDITIONAL PROVISION)**, Dec 2025.
  Sections 1-9 are the Apache License 2.0; Section 10 is added. Full text:
  https://github.com/PriorLabs/TabPFN/blob/main/LICENSE (a copy is placed in
  `third_party/tabpfn/LICENSE` by the install script).

Section 10, quoted verbatim from the upstream `LICENSE`:

> 10. Additional attribution.
> If You distribute or make available the Work or any Derivative
> Work thereof relating to any part of the source or model weights,
> or a product or service (including another AI model) that contains
> any source or model weights, You shall (A) provide a copy of this
> License with any such materials; and (B) prominently display
> “Built with PriorLabs-TabPFN” on each related website, user interface, blogpost,
> about page, or product documentation. If You use the source or model
> weights or model outputs to create, train, fine tune, distil, or
> otherwise improve an AI model, which is distributed or made available,
> you shall also include “TabPFN” at the beginning of any such AI model name.
> To clarify, internal benchmarking and testing without external
> communication shall not qualify as distribution or making available
> pursuant to this Section 10 and no attribution under this Section 10
> shall be required.

What this requires of this repository (our patch is a Derivative Work relating to the source):

1. Ship a copy of the Prior Labs License with the patch (Section 10(A); also Apache 4(a)).
   -> `patches/tabpfn/LICENSE` must be present in the release.
2. Prominently display **“Built with PriorLabs-TabPFN”** in the repository README / project
   page and in any related website or blog post (Section 10(B)).
3. Modified files must carry prominent notices that they were changed (Apache 4(b)); the
   patch headers and `patches/README.md` serve this purpose.
4. If we ever distribute a *model* fine-tuned from TabPFN (e.g. a DiagScale delta or a
   fine-tuned checkpoint), its name must begin with “TabPFN” (Section 10, last sentence).
   This release distributes no such model.

TabPFN-3 model weights (`Prior-Labs/tabpfn_3`, used by default by `tabpfn` v8) are **not**
covered by the code license: they are under the **TABPFN-3 License v1.0** (non-commercial;
https://huggingface.co/Prior-Labs/tabpfn_3/blob/main/LICENSE). Users obtain them directly from
Prior Labs (the library asks the user to accept the license and uses a `TABPFN_TOKEN`). That
license allows distribution of Derivatives (e.g. fine-tuned weights) only for non-commercial
purposes, with the license copy, the attribution notice “The TABPFN-3 Model is licensed by
Prior Labs GmbH under the TABPFN-3 Non-Commercial License. Copyright © Prior Labs GmbH 2026.”,
a statement of modification, and no hosted/API service (Section 3). It also restricts using
Outputs to train a model competitive with TabPFN-3 (Section 2(d)).

## TabICL (Soda team, Inria)

- Upstream: https://github.com/soda-inria/tabicl (base: commit `46b91961` on `main`, between v2.1.1 and v2.2.0)
- Patch: `patches/tabicl/`, `patches/tabicl.diff`
- License: **BSD 3-Clause**, Copyright (c) 2025, Soda team @ Inria. Redistribution of the
  patch must retain the copyright notice, conditions and disclaimer
  (`patches/tabicl/LICENSE`); the Inria name may not be used to endorse derived products.
- Weights: `jingang/TabICL` on the Hugging Face Hub, tagged `bsd-3-clause`.

## TabFM (Google LLC)

- Upstream: https://github.com/google-research/tabfm (base: commit `2fb67e86` on `main`)
- Patch: `patches/tabfm/`, `patches/tabfm.diff`
- Code license: **Apache License 2.0**, Copyright 2026 Google LLC. Keep the license copy
  (`patches/tabfm/LICENSE`) and per-file headers; modified files are marked by the patch.
- Weights: `google/tabfm-1.0.0-pytorch` on the Hugging Face Hub. The model card states:
  “The model weights in this repository are released under the **TabFM Non-Commercial License
  v1.0** - see [LICENSE](https://huggingface.co/google/tabfm-1.0.0-pytorch/blob/main/LICENSE).
  The source code is Apache 2.0 licensed via google-research/tabfm.” The weights license
  permits non-commercial research use only and, in Section 3(b), prohibits distributing the
  TabFM Model **or any Derivative** (fine-tuned / adapted versions included). This repository
  therefore must not ship TabFM weights or fine-tuned TabFM adapters/checkpoints.

## TabArena (AutoGluon)

- Upstream: https://github.com/autogluon/tabarena (pinned at commit `abd24c7f`, unmodified;
  `packages/tabarena` and `packages/bencheval` are installed)
- License: **Apache License 2.0**. TabArena pulls in AutoGluon (Apache-2.0) and other
  dependencies under their own licenses.

FILE: configs/tabpfn3/aggregation_96.yaml
================================================
# TabPFN-3 configuration pool + greedy selection (+67 Elo).
# 96 configurations sampled with seed 0 from the TabPFN search space (configuration 0 = default),
# each evaluated with 8-fold out-of-fold predictions at 2 views and on the test split at 8 views;
# 40 rounds of greedy ensemble selection with replacement on the out-of-fold loss.
# Per-cell member predictions are written to <eval>/agg_preds/ and costs to agg_cost.jsonl.
model: tabpfn3-agg
mode: full
tag_suffix: aggregation_96
hyperparameters:
  aggregation:
    n_configs: 96
    seed: 0
    n_est_val: 2
    n_est_test: 8
    val_folds: 8
    n_iterations: 40



================================================

# TabPFN-3 DiagScale (+26 Elo).

# weights stay frozen. Learning rate 3e-2, up to 30 epochs; other settings as in full_ft.yaml.
model: tabpfn3-ft
mode: full
tag_suffix: diagscale

  ft_lr: 3.0e-2
  ft_epochs: 30

================================================
FILE: docs/evaluation.md
================================================
# Evaluation protocol

All numbers in the paper come from TabArena per-cell results (`results_per_split.csv`, one row
per dataset x fold x method) and two computations implemented in `ttc.eval`.

## Benchmark

TabArena: 51 datasets, 816 cells (dataset x repeat x fold; TabArena's `fold` column enumerates
repeat x fold). Cell error `metric_error` is 1 - AUC (binary), log loss (multiclass) or RMSE
(regression). Cells a method did not produce are imputed with `RF (default)`, as TabArena does.

## Elo (`ttc.eval.elo`)

* **Fixed reference field, 68 entries:** the 67 official TabArena entries plus our frozen TabFM
  reproduction (run `repro_tabfm_bf16-cellcap`, entry `Frozen_TabFM_reference`).
* **One candidate per fit:** each candidate is inserted alone (69 entries). Candidates are never
  fit together, so adding an arm never moves another arm's number.
* **Solver:** TabArena's Bradley-Terry MLE (`bencheval.elo_utils.EloHelper.compute_mle_elo`) on
  pairwise per-cell wins/losses/ties, each cell weighted by 1/C_d (C_d = cells of dataset d),
  calibrated so `RF (default)` = 1000. Weighted pair totals are passed as one battle row per
  outcome, which is the exact sufficient statistic of TabArena's per-cell battles (tested).
* **Delta Elo:** Elo(candidate) - Elo(baseline) within the same fit, computed unrounded and
  rounded once. Baselines: `TA-TABPFN-3 (default)` (TabPFN-3 arms), `TABICLV2 (default)`
  (TabICL v2 arms), `Frozen_TabFM_reference` (TabFM arms).
* **Normalized score:** TabArena's: per dataset, mean cell error rescaled to
  1 - clip((e - best)/(median - best), 0, 1) over the 69 entries, averaged over datasets.

```bash
python -m ttc.eval.elo \
    --candidate hf_results/raw/tabpfn3_diagscale/results_per_split.csv \
    --reference-field hf_results/raw/tabfm_frozen/results_per_split.csv \
    --baseline "TA-TABPFN-3 (default)"
# baseline: TA-TABPFN-3 (default)
#  name  elo          baseline_elo  delta_elo  delta_rounded  score     n_entries  reference_source
#  ...   1681.940184  1656.275317   25.664867  26             0.698254  69         tabarena
```

Repeat `--candidate` (and optionally `--name`) to evaluate several arms; each is fit separately.
`--show Frozen_TabFM_reference` also prints another entry's rating in each fit (the paper reads
the frozen TabFM rating from the TabFM full-FT fit). `--json` prints one JSON object per candidate.

**Where the 67 references come from** (`--references`, default `auto` = first available):

| value | source |
|---|---|
| `field` | the `--reference-field` CSV, if it is a full TabArena board (as in the paper) |
| `candidate` | the candidate CSV, if it is a full TabArena board (a `compare` run embeds them) |
| `tabarena` | TabArena itself: `TabArenaContext().load_results_paper()` (downloaded once into TabArena's cache), RF-imputed with `tabarena.nips2025_utils.compare.prepare_data` |
| a path | any TabArena long-format CSV/parquet containing the 67 entries |

All routes give bit-identical reference matrices; when several are present they are checked
against each other. The released results contain only our rows, so they use the `tabarena` route.

API:

```python
from ttc.eval.elo import evaluate_candidate
r = evaluate_candidate("cand.csv", "frozen_tabfm.csv", baseline="TABICLV2 (default)")
r.elo, r.delta_elo, r.delta_rounded, r.score, r.board  # board = Elo + score of all 69 entries
```

Lower-level pieces: `error_matrix`, `reference_matrix`, `build_field`, `fit_elo`,
`normalized_scores`.

## Paired error change (`ttc.eval.paired`)

For candidate m and baseline b on the same cells, l_d = mean over the cells of dataset d of
log(e_m / e_b). Reported: 100 (exp(mean_d l_d) - 1) %, a 95% percentile bootstrap interval over
datasets (10,000 resamples, `numpy.random.default_rng(0)`), and wins = #datasets with l_d < 0.

```bash
python -m ttc.eval.paired \
    --candidate hf_results/raw/tabfm_diagscale/results_per_split.csv \
    --baseline  hf_results/raw/tabfm_full_ft/results_per_split.csv
# error change +0.102%  95% CI [-0.054%, +0.286%]  wins 28/51  (816 cells)
```

`--baseline-method "TA-TABPFN-3 (default)"` compares against a reference row of a full board
(`--baseline` then defaults to the candidate CSV).

## Reproducing the paper

`tests/test_eval_elo.py` checks the 12 headline arms (Elo, Delta Elo to 1e-6, normalized score)
and the DiagScale-vs-full-FT paired table on all three backbones:

```bash
TTC_HF_RESULTS_DIR=hf_results pytest tests/test_eval_elo.py
```

`TTC_HF_RESULTS_DIR` is the downloaded Hugging Face folder (`hf_results/raw/<arm>/results_per_split.csv`,
candidate rows only; TabArena must be installed to supply the references).
`TTC_PAPER_RESULTS_DIR` instead points at `<run tag>/results_per_split.csv` of full TabArena boards
from your own runs. Tests skip when the data are absent.

## Releasing results

`scripts/export_hf_results.py` writes the Hugging Face dataset folder: `data/results.parquet` (all
12 arms, one row per arm x cell), `data/arms.parquet` and `data/arms.csv` (one row per arm: config,
Elo, Delta Elo, GPU hours, sha256), `raw/<arm>/results_per_split.csv` (the arm's candidate rows,
copied byte-for-byte; `raw/tabfm_frozen` is the 68th reference) and the `README.md` dataset card.
It does not upload.



================================================
FILE: docs/retrieval.md
================================================
# Large-table row retrieval (model-aware vs feed-all)

Frozen TabPFN-3, single view. Two arms on a fixed 50,000-row test set per seed:

* **feed-all** (`--arms full`): the whole source pool of N rows as context.
* **model-aware** (`--arms modelaware`): attention-guided retrieval. Each query's attention over
  the pool (last ICL layer, query-to-context softmax, mean over heads) gives its top r = 500 rows;
  queries are clustered by their binary retrieval indicators
  (G = min(n_Q, max(4, min(30, floor(n_Q/2000)))) = 25 KMeans clusters, random_state 0, n_init 4);
  each cluster predicts with the union of its queries' rows.

Beyond memory (feed-all fails at 4.0-4.5M rows on an 80 GB GPU), the shuffled pool is scored in
2M-row blocks (`--shard-size 2000000`) with proportional per-block quotas (largest-remainder
rounding) and within-block ranks. Per-cluster unions are capped at C = 4M rows
(`--union-size 4000000`, alias `--union-cap`); `--overflow` decides what happens above C:

* `truncate` (default): keep the C rows with the largest summed retained attention.
* `split`: recursively bisect the cluster (2-center KMeans on its queries' binary indicators,
  queries ordered by distance difference, balanced halves) until every union fits; at most
  `--max-clusters 64` clusters. Uses no labels. Paper: Higgs at 6M-10M.

Code: `ttc.benchmarks.row_modelaware` (pipeline + CLI), `ttc.benchmarks.row_coreset_large`
(data loading, per-seed split, feed-all arm), `ttc.benchmarks.row_pool_prep` (pool preparation),
`ttc.retrieval.host_probe` (streaming attention top-k).

## Environment

| Variable | Default | Used for |
|---|---|---|
| `TTC_DATA_DIR` | `./data/row_large` | prepared npy pools |
| `TTC_RUNS_DIR` | `./runs` | default output, `$TTC_RUNS_DIR/row_modelaware/cells.csv` |
| `OPENML_CACHE_DIR` | openml's default | OpenML downloads (read by openml itself) |
| `TABPFN_MODEL_CACHE_DIR` | tabpfn's default | TabPFN-3 checkpoint, downloaded by tabpfn on first use |

The TabPFN-3 checkpoint is tabpfn's default (`tabpfn-v3-classifier-v3_default.ckpt`); nothing in
these modules points to a local path. Run everything from the repository root with the package
installed (`pip install -e .`) or `PYTHONPATH=src`.

## 1. Prepare the pools (CPU)

Covertype (OpenML 1596) and Poker-Hand (OpenML 1567) are read from OpenML at run time. The three
tables beyond 1M rows are converted once:

```bash
# full tables: Higgs (OpenML 45570, 11M), COMET_MC (5889, 7.62M), US_Accidents (46650, 7.73M)
python -m ttc.benchmarks.row_pool_prep --datasets Higgs COMET_MC US_Accidents
# plus the 3M-row subsets used by the within-memory sweep (up to 2.9M rows)
python -m ttc.benchmarks.row_pool_prep --datasets Higgs COMET_MC US_Accidents \
    --skip-full --subset-rows 3000000
```

This writes `{Higgs,COMET_MC,US_Accidents}_full_{X,y}.npy` + `_meta.json` and
`{...}_3M_{X,y}.npy` + `_meta.json` into `$TTC_DATA_DIR`. The subset is
`full[np.random.default_rng(0).choice(N_full, 3_000_000, replace=False)]` in draw order.

No split files are written. For seed s, `row_coreset_large._split` permutes the loaded table with
`np.random.default_rng(s)`; the first 50,000 rows are the test set and a pool of size N is the
first N of the remaining rows. Each table family (full, 3M subset, OpenML) therefore has its own
seed-specific test set, as in the paper.

## 2. Smoke test (one GPU, minutes)

```bash
python -m ttc.benchmarks.row_modelaware --datasets Covertype --n-avail 20000 --seeds 0 \
    --arms full modelaware --out runs/row_modelaware/smoke.csv
# exercises the split policy (unions of 2,000 queries x 500 rows exceed a 15k cap)
python -m ttc.benchmarks.row_modelaware --datasets Covertype --n-avail 20000 --seeds 0 \
    --arms modelaware --union-size 15000 --overflow split --out runs/row_modelaware/smoke_split.csv
```

`python -m ttc.benchmarks.row_modelaware --smoke` runs the CPU logic checks (streaming top-k equals
the full attention map, block quotas and offsets, truncation, splitting, CSV output) on a
7,000-row Covertype sample. `tests/test_retrieval_split.py` checks the split policy alone (CPU).

## 3. Paper settings

All runs: seeds 0-4, `--n-est 1` (default), `--retrieval-len 500` (default),
`--union-size 4000000` (default; never binds within memory). Paper results come from separate
jobs per dataset and seed group; the commands below give the grid.

**Within memory, unsharded** ("Within memory" columns of the paper's row-construction table;
row-curation figures):

```bash
python -m ttc.benchmarks.row_modelaware --datasets Covertype --seeds 0 1 2 3 4 \
    --n-avail 100000 300000 500000 --arms full modelaware
python -m ttc.benchmarks.row_modelaware --datasets Poker --seeds 0 1 2 3 4 \
    --n-avail 100000 300000 500000 900000 --arms full modelaware
python -m ttc.benchmarks.row_modelaware --datasets Higgs_3M --seeds 0 1 2 3 4 \
    --n-avail 300000 500000 900000 1500000 2000000 2500000 2900000 --arms full modelaware
python -m ttc.benchmarks.row_modelaware --datasets US_Accidents_3M COMET_MC_3M --seeds 0 1 2 3 4 \
    --n-avail 300000 900000 1500000 2000000 2500000 2900000 --arms full modelaware
```

(The paper's model-aware Higgs subset sweep has no 2.5M point; feed-all does.)

**Full tables up to the wall, unsharded** (feed-all references at 2M and 4M):

```bash
python -m ttc.benchmarks.row_modelaware --datasets Higgs_full US_Accidents_full COMET_MC_full \
    --seeds 0 1 2 3 4 --n-avail 2000000 4000000 --arms full modelaware
```

**Two-block scoring control at 4M** (paper appendix "Reaching past the memory wall"):

```bash
python -m ttc.benchmarks.row_modelaware --datasets Higgs_full US_Accidents_full COMET_MC_full \
    --seeds 0 1 2 3 4 --n-avail 4000000 --arms modelaware --shard-size 2000000
```

**Beyond the wall** (sharded scoring, 2M blocks; feed-all is not run, its reference is 4M):

```bash
# Higgs: recursive split (paper main result) ...
python -m ttc.benchmarks.row_modelaware --datasets Higgs_full --seeds 0 1 2 3 4 \
    --n-avail 6000000 8000000 10000000 --arms modelaware \
    --shard-size 2000000 --union-size 4000000 --overflow split --max-clusters 64
# ... and the capped-union control (Higgs union-capacity table)
python -m ttc.benchmarks.row_modelaware --datasets Higgs_full --seeds 0 1 2 3 4 \
    --n-avail 6000000 8000000 10000000 --arms modelaware \
    --shard-size 2000000 --union-size 4000000 --overflow truncate
# US_Accidents (7,678,394 = whole pool) and COMET_MC (7,569,400 = whole pool): truncate
python -m ttc.benchmarks.row_modelaware --datasets US_Accidents_full --seeds 0 1 2 3 4 \
    --n-avail 6000000 7678394 --arms modelaware --shard-size 2000000 --union-size 4000000
python -m ttc.benchmarks.row_modelaware --datasets COMET_MC_full --seeds 0 1 2 3 4 \
    --n-avail 6000000 7569400 --arms modelaware --shard-size 2000000 --union-size 4000000
```

Relative log-loss reduction is `100 * (err_feedall - err_modelaware) / err_feedall`, paired by
dataset, N and seed; beyond the wall the reference is feed-all at 4M on the same full-table seed.

Output columns: `err` (log loss), `auc` (binary), `acc`, `secs`, `peak_gb`, `coverage` (captured
attention mass), `union_med`/`union_max` (per-cluster context sizes), `n_shards`, `n_capped`
and `trunc_max` (truncated clusters and largest truncated fraction), `n_leaf_clusters` and
`n_splits` (final clusters and splits under `--overflow split`). `k_clusters` is the initial
cluster count G.

## Notes on reproducibility

* **Split policy.** The paper's Higgs 6M-10M split runs used the same algorithm applied as a
  patch around the same pipeline (25 initial clusters, C = 4M, at most 64 clusters).
  `--overflow split` integrates it; `tests/test_retrieval_split.py` checks that cluster
  assignments are identical. Those runs report 26-27 final clusters. Seed 0 informed the
  development of the split rule.
* **3M subsets.** The subset builder reproduces the paper's Higgs and COMET_MC 3M subsets
  bit for bit. The paper's US_Accidents 3M subset was drawn by a different, unrecorded procedure,
  so US_Accidents results at 2.9M rows and below come from a different (equally random) subset.
* **Hardware.** Feed-all at 4M rows needs about 80 GB of GPU memory (about 20 GB per 1M rows).
  `--buf-gb` (default 8) bounds the scratch memory of the attention-scoring pass.
* Model-aware scoring and clustering see the whole unlabeled test batch at once, so contexts
  depend on which queries are evaluated together; test labels are used only for scoring.

FILE: patches/README.md

# Backbone patches

The experiments use small, additive patches on top of three public tabular foundation
model libraries. Each package has

- `patches/<pkg>/NNNN-*.patch`: a `git format-patch` series (commit messages and authorship
  kept; apply with `git am`), and
- `patches/<pkg>.diff`: the same change as one squashed diff (`git apply --3way`), and
- `patches/<pkg>/LICENSE`: the upstream license at the base commit (the patches are
  derivative works distributed under it; see `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md`).

`scripts/install_forks.sh` clones each upstream at the base commit, applies the series,
**checks that the resulting source tree is bit-identical to the one used for the paper**
(git tree hash), and installs it with `pip install -e`.

| package | upstream | base commit | patch range | result tree (= paper fork commit) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `tabpfn` | https://github.com/PriorLabs/TabPFN | `95ff6772` = tag **v8.0.7** | 8 commits, `95ff6772..bf9b2c99` | `f2094982` (fork `bf9b2c99`) |
| `tabicl` | https://github.com/soda-inria/tabicl | `46b91961` on `main` (after v2.1.1, before v2.2.0) | 3 commits, `46b9196..efda645` | `2df5bffc` (fork `efda645`) |
| `tabfm` | https://github.com/google-research/tabfm | `2fb67e86` on `main` (before v1.0.1) | 3 commits, `2fb67e8..78beef7` | `7abb8157` (fork `78beef7`) |
| `tabarena` | https://github.com/autogluon/tabarena | `abd24c7f` on `main` | none (pinned only) | — |

All base commits are reachable from the public upstream `main` branch (checked against the
upstream remotes). Every patch only touches the files listed below; none changes default
inference behaviour of the unpatched library except where noted (tabfm bf16 path).

## tabpfn (Prior Labs TabPFN, v8.0.7 + 8 commits)

Files: `src/tabpfn/finetuning/{peft.py (new), finetuned_base.py, finetuned_classifier.py, finetuned_regressor.py}`

1. **PEFT parameter-selection hook.** `finetune_mode` / `finetune_cfg` constructor arguments on
   `FinetunedTabPFNClassifier/Regressor`; `finetuning/peft.py::select_trainable` chooses which
   parameters the optimizer trains (`full` = exact upstream behaviour, `bitfit`, `regex`).
2. **Epoch bookkeeping** exposed after `fit`: `epochs_run_`, `best_epoch_`, `stopped_early_`;
   later also `best_metric_`, `greater_is_better_` (used by the per-task learning-rate sweep).
3. **`auto_scale_n_estimators` disabled in the finetuning training forward only.** Upstream
   scales the ensemble up on wide tables, which breaks the finetuning loss's assertion that
   exactly `n_estimators_finetune` members are returned. Validation and final inference keep
   auto-scaling.
4. **Fall-back to base weights.** With early stopping on, if no epoch beats the
   pre-finetuning validation score (`best_epoch_ == -1`) the base weights are restored instead
   of shipping the last (degraded) epoch.
5. **LoRA mode** (`finetune_mode="lora"`): `LoRALinear` / `inject_lora`, zero-initialised `B`,
   seeded adapter init (reproducible under a fixed `random_state`), guard rails for `r<=0`.
6. **DiagScale mode** (`finetune_mode="diagscale"`): `DiagScale` / `inject_diagscale` wrap the
   attention `SoftmaxScalingMLP` and multiply its output query by a trainable zero-initialised
   per-(head, dim) factor `(1 + delta)`, i.e. a learned diagonal similarity metric.

Excluded on purpose: fork commit `2102d854` (opt-in step-granular validation) and
uncommitted research edits; they are not used by any released result.

License: Prior Labs License v1.2 (Apache-2.0 + additional attribution provision, see
`THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md`). The TabPFN-3 model weights are under the separate
non-commercial TABPFN-3 License v1.0 and are downloaded by `tabpfn` itself; we do not
redistribute them.

## tabicl (TabICL v2, 46b9196 + 3 commits)

Files: `src/tabicl/_model/{attention.py, layers.py, peft.py (new)}`, `tests/test_peft.py (new)`

1. **DiagScale and (IA)^3 adapters.** `MultiheadAttention` gains `diag_delta`,
   `ia3_delta_k`, `ia3_delta_v` slots (plain `None` attributes, so released checkpoints still
   load with `strict=True`); `MultiheadAttentionBlock` gains `ia3_delta_ff` on the FFN
   activation. `_model/peft.py` provides `inject_diagscale`, `inject_ia3`, `select_trainable`
   (`full | regex | diagscale | ia3`). Bit-exact identity at `delta = 0`.
2. **`freeze_others` flag** on `select_trainable` (flipping `requires_grad` changes the CUDA
   train-mode forward by ~5e-3; `freeze_others=False` keeps flag parity).
3. **KV-cache fix:** the cache now stores the pre-(IA)^3 K/V (previously the rescale was
   applied twice on cache reuse); `select_trainable` hardening. 12 unit tests.

License: BSD-3-Clause (Soda team @ Inria). The TabICL checkpoints (`jingang/TabICL` on the
Hugging Face Hub, fetched by the library) are also tagged BSD-3-Clause.

## tabfm (Google TabFM, 2fb67e8 + 3 commits)

Files: `tabfm/src/classifier_and_regressor.py`, `tabfm/src/pytorch/{model.py, peft.py (new), peft_test.py (new)}`

1. **bf16 inference.** The PyTorch predict step casts inputs to the model's parameter dtype
   instead of hard-coded float32 (fp32 models unchanged) and casts logits back to fp32 before
   NumPy.
2. **DiagScale adapter:** zero-initialised `delta[nhead, head_dim]` multiplying the final query
   (after RoPE / q-RMSNorm / per-dim scale), `select_trainable(full | regex | diagscale)`.
3. **(IA)^3 baseline mode:** `1 + delta` on keys / values / FFN activation.

Excluded on purpose: fork commit `bdbe4ae` (per-head temperature adapter), not used by any
released result.

License: code Apache-2.0 (Google LLC). The TabFM model weights
(`google/tabfm-1.0.0-pytorch` on Hugging Face) are under the separate **TabFM Non-Commercial
License v1.0**, which forbids distributing the model or any Derivative (including fine-tuned
weights); we do not redistribute weights or fine-tuned adapters.

## tabarena (autogluon/tabarena @ abd24c7, no patch)

Only `packages/bencheval` and `packages/tabarena` are installed (editable). tabarena requires
`autogluon>=1.5,<1.6`. License: Apache-2.0.

## Regenerating the patches (maintainers)

```bash
git -C <tabpfn-fork> format-patch 95ff6772..bf9b2c99 -o patches/tabpfn
git -C <tabpfn-fork> diff 95ff6772 bf9b2c99 > patches/tabpfn.diff
git -C <tabicl-fork> format-patch 46b9196..efda645 -o patches/tabicl
git -C <tabicl-fork> diff 46b9196 efda645 > patches/tabicl.diff
git -C <tabfm-fork>  format-patch 2fb67e8..78beef7 -o patches/tabfm
git -C <tabfm-fork>  diff 2fb67e8 78beef7 > patches/tabfm.diff
```

+"""Parameter-selection hook for PEFT-style finetuning of the PyTorch TabFM
+(ttc diagscale fork addition, mirroring the tabpfn fork's finetuning/peft.py).
+
+select_trainable() decides which parameters a finetuning optimizer sees;
+everything else is frozen (requires_grad=False).
+
+Modes:
+    full       all parameters, requires_grad untouched (plain full finetuning)
+    regex      parameters whose fully-qualified name matches cfg["pattern"]
+    diagscale  inject a fresh per-(head,dim) multiplier 1+delta on the query of
+               every MultiheadAttention whose MODULE name matches
+               cfg["pattern"], then train ONLY the deltas. delta is
+               zero-initialized so injection is behavior-preserving (the
+               wrapped model reproduces the pretrained model exactly until
+               training moves delta). The multiplier is applied to the final
+               query right before SDPA (after RoPE / q-RMSNorm /
+               per_dim_scale; SDPA runs at scale=1.0), so it is literally a
+               learned diagonal similarity metric: q̃·k = qᵀ diag(1+δ) k.
+    ia3        (IA)3 (Liu et al. 2022) baseline: 1+delta multipliers on keys /
+               values (post key_ln) and/or the FFN intermediate activation,
+               cfg["parts"] ⊆ {"k","v","ff"} (default all three). Same zero-init
+               delta convention as diagscale so lr/wd sweeps are comparable;
+               the k part is logit-equivalent to diagscale's q-side delta.
+
+Useful patterns (module names of the released TabFM v1.0.0):
+    "."                  all 42 attention modules (53,760 delta params)
+    r"^col_embedder\\."   the FIRST column embedder only — the most upstream
+                         attention that reads the training set (it embeds each
+                         feature's distribution by attending over train rows);
+                         6 modules, 1,536 delta params.
+"""
+from __future__ import annotations
+
+import logging
+import re
+from typing import Any
+
+import torch
+
+from tabfm.src.pytorch.model import MultiheadAttention, MultiheadAttentionBlock
+
+logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
+
+
+def inject_diagscale(model: torch.nn.Module, pattern: str) -> int:
+  """Register a zero-init diag_delta [nhead, hd] on every MultiheadAttention
+  whose module name matches `pattern`. In-place; returns the number injected.
+
+  delta is created float32 regardless of the backbone dtype (the forward casts
+  1+delta to the compute dtype), so tiny optimizer updates are not lost to
+  bf16 rounding. No RNG needed (deterministic zero-init).
+  """
+  rx = re.compile(pattern)
+  targets = [(n, m) for n, m in model.named_modules()
+             if isinstance(m, MultiheadAttention) and rx.search(n)]
+  for name, mod in targets:
+    if mod.diag_delta is not None:
+      raise ValueError(f"diagscale already injected at {name!r}")
+    ref = mod.per_dim_scale
+    mod.diag_delta = torch.nn.Parameter(
+        torch.zeros(mod.nhead, mod.hd, device=ref.device, dtype=torch.float32))
+  return len(targets)
+
+
+def inject_ia3(model: torch.nn.Module, pattern: str, parts: tuple = ("k", "v", "ff")) -> int:
+  """(IA)3 (Liu et al. 2022) adapters as zero-init deltas: 1+delta multipliers on keys /
+  values ([nhead, hd], applied post key_ln — the metric-exact analog of diag_delta on q)
+  and/or the FFN intermediate activation ([dim_ff]). `pattern` matches module names;
+  `parts` selects which of k / v / ff to inject. Returns the number of injected tensors.
+
+  Zero-init deltas (rather than the paper's ones-init vectors) keep the multiplier
+  identity at start AND give AdamW weight decay the correct prior (pull toward the
+  pretrained model, not toward a zero function) — consistent with diagscale, so lr/wd
+  sweeps are directly comparable across the two adapter families.
+  """
+  rx = re.compile(pattern)
+  n = 0
+  if "k" in parts or "v" in parts:
+    for name, mod in model.named_modules():
+      if isinstance(mod, MultiheadAttention) and rx.search(name):
+        ref = mod.per_dim_scale
+        if "k" in parts:
+          if mod.ia3_delta_k is not None:
+            raise ValueError(f"ia3 (k) already injected at {name!r}")
+          mod.ia3_delta_k = torch.nn.Parameter(
+              torch.zeros(mod.nhead, mod.hd, device=ref.device, dtype=torch.float32))
+          n += 1
+        if "v" in parts:
+          if mod.ia3_delta_v is not None:
+            raise ValueError(f"ia3 (v) already injected at {name!r}")
+          mod.ia3_delta_v = torch.nn.Parameter(
+              torch.zeros(mod.nhead, mod.hd, device=ref.device, dtype=torch.float32))
+          n += 1
+  if "ff" in parts:
+    for name, mod in model.named_modules():
+      if isinstance(mod, MultiheadAttentionBlock) and rx.search(name):
+        if mod.ia3_delta_ff is not None:
+          raise ValueError(f"ia3 (ff) already injected at {name!r}")
+        ref = mod.linear2.weight
+        mod.ia3_delta_ff = torch.nn.Parameter(
+            torch.zeros(mod.linear2.in_features, device=ref.device, dtype=torch.float32))
+        n += 1
+  return n
+
+
+def select_trainable(
+    model: torch.nn.Module,
+    mode: str = "full",
+    **cfg: Any,
+) -> list[torch.nn.Parameter]:
+  """Freeze/unfreeze `model`'s parameters per `mode`; return the trainables."""
+  named = list(model.named_parameters())
+  n_total = sum(p.numel() for _, p in named)
+
+  if mode == "full":
+    selected = [p for _, p in named]  # requires_grad untouched
+  elif mode == "diagscale":
+    n_injected = inject_diagscale(model, pattern=cfg["pattern"])
+    if n_injected == 0:
+      raise ValueError(
+          f"finetune_mode='diagscale' (cfg={cfg}) matched 0 MultiheadAttention modules")
+    selected = []
+    for name, param in model.named_parameters():  # re-enumerate: deltas now exist
+      train = name.endswith(".diag_delta")
+      param.requires_grad_(train)
+      if train:
+        selected.append(param)
+    if not selected:
+      raise ValueError(
+          f"finetune_mode='diagscale' injected {n_injected} module(s) but selected 0 params")
+    logger.info("finetune_mode=diagscale: injected %d attention module(s)", n_injected)
+  elif mode == "ia3":
+    parts = tuple(cfg.get("parts", ("k", "v", "ff")))
+    n_injected = inject_ia3(model, pattern=cfg.get("pattern", "."), parts=parts)
+    if n_injected == 0:
+      raise ValueError(f"finetune_mode='ia3' (cfg={cfg}) matched 0 modules")
+    selected = []
+    for name, param in model.named_parameters():  # re-enumerate: deltas now exist
+      train = any(name.endswith(f".ia3_delta_{p}") for p in ("k", "v", "ff"))
+      param.requires_grad_(train)
+      if train:
+        selected.append(param)
+    if not selected:
+      raise ValueError(f"finetune_mode='ia3' injected {n_injected} tensor(s) but selected 0 params")
+    logger.info("finetune_mode=ia3: injected %d tensor(s) (parts=%s)", n_injected, parts)
+  elif mode == "regex":
+    rx = re.compile(cfg["pattern"])
+    selected = []
+    for name, param in named:
+      train = rx.search(name) is not None
+      param.requires_grad_(train)
+      if train:
+        selected.append(param)
+    if not selected:
+      raise ValueError(f"finetune_mode='regex' (cfg={cfg}) selected 0 parameters")
+  else:
+    raise ValueError(f"unknown finetune_mode {mode!r} (full | regex | diagscale | ia3)")
+
+  n_selected = sum(p.numel() for p in selected)
+  msg = (f"finetune_mode={mode}: trainable {n_selected:,} / {n_total:,} params "
+         f"({100 * n_selected / n_total:.4f}%)")
+  logger.info(msg)
+  print(f"[tabfm.pytorch.peft] {msg}", flush=True)  # visibility in job logs
+  return selected

index 0000000..bc40ec1

+# Copyright 2026 Google LLC
+#
+# Licensed under the Apache License, Version 2.0 (the "License");
+# you may not use this file except in compliance with the License.
+# You may obtain a copy of the License at
+#
+#     https://www.apache.org/licenses/LICENSE-2.0
+#
+# Unless required by applicable law or agreed to in writing, software
+# distributed under the License is distributed on an "AS IS" BASIS,
+# WITHOUT WARRANTIES OR CONDITIONS OF ANY KIND, either express or implied.
+# See the License for the specific language governing permissions and
+# limitations under the License.
+
+import unittest
+
+import torch
+
+from tabfm.src.pytorch import peft
+from tabfm.src.pytorch.model import MultiheadAttention, TabFM
+
+
+def _tiny_model(seed=0):
+  torch.manual_seed(seed)
+  m = TabFM(
+      embed_dim=16, max_classes=10, col_num_blocks=2, col_nhead=2,
+      col_num_inds=8, row_num_blocks=2, row_nhead=2, row_num_cls=4,
+      icl_num_blocks=2, icl_nhead=2, ff_factor=2, feature_group_size=3,
+      is_classifier=True)
+  # The released checkpoints have non-trivial weights; randomize the zeros-init
+  # params so the identity test is not trivially passing on zero activations.
+  with torch.no_grad():
+    for p in m.parameters():
+      if (p == 0).all():
+        p.normal_(0, 0.02)
+  m.eval()
+  return m
+
+
+def _dummy_batch(seed=0):
+  g = torch.Generator().manual_seed(seed)
+  x = torch.randn(2, 6, 5, generator=g)
+  y = torch.randint(0, 3, (2, 6), generator=g)
+  train_size = torch.tensor([4, 4])
+  return x, y, train_size
+
+
+class PeftTest(unittest.TestCase):
+
+  def test_injection_is_identity_at_init(self):
+    """Zero-init delta must not change the forward output at all."""
+    m = _tiny_model()
+    x, y, train_size = _dummy_batch()
+    with torch.no_grad():
+      before = m(x, y, train_size)
+    n = peft.inject_diagscale(m, pattern=".")
+    self.assertGreater(n, 0)
+    with torch.no_grad():
+      after = m(x, y, train_size)
+    torch.testing.assert_close(before, after, rtol=0, atol=0)
+
+  def test_select_trainable_diagscale_only_deltas(self):
+    m = _tiny_model()
+    n_attn = sum(1 for _, mod in m.named_modules()
+                 if isinstance(mod, MultiheadAttention))
+    selected = peft.select_trainable(m, mode="diagscale", pattern=".")
+    self.assertEqual(len(selected), n_attn)
+    for name, p in m.named_parameters():
+      self.assertEqual(p.requires_grad, name.endswith(".diag_delta"), name)
+    n_expected = sum(mod.nhead * mod.hd for _, mod in m.named_modules()
+                     if isinstance(mod, MultiheadAttention))
+    self.assertEqual(sum(p.numel() for p in selected), n_expected)
+
+  def test_scoped_pattern_matches_subset(self):
+    m = _tiny_model()
+    n = peft.inject_diagscale(m, pattern=r"^col_embedder\.")
+    # col_embedder: col_num_blocks x (mab1, mab2) = 4 attention modules.
+    self.assertEqual(n, 4)
+    injected = [name for name, _ in m.named_parameters()
+                if name.endswith(".diag_delta")]
+    self.assertEqual(len(injected), 4)
+    for name in injected:
+      self.assertTrue(name.startswith("col_embedder."), name)
+
+  def test_gradient_flows_to_delta_and_moves_output(self):
+    m = _tiny_model()
+    selected = peft.select_trainable(m, mode="diagscale", pattern=".")
+    x, y, train_size = _dummy_batch()
+    out = m(x, y, train_size)
+    loss = out.square().mean()
+    loss.backward()
+    grads = [p.grad for p in selected]
+    self.assertTrue(all(g is not None for g in grads))
+    self.assertTrue(any(g.abs().sum() > 0 for g in grads))
+    # A non-zero delta must change the output (the multiplier is live).
+    with torch.no_grad():
+      before = m(x, y, train_size)
+      for p in selected:
+        p.add_(0.05)
+      after = m(x, y, train_size)
+    self.assertGreater((before - after).abs().max().item(), 0)
+
+  def test_full_mode_untouched(self):
+    m = _tiny_model()
+    selected = peft.select_trainable(m, mode="full")
+    self.assertEqual(len(selected), len(list(m.parameters())))
+    self.assertTrue(all(p.requires_grad for p in m.parameters()))
+
+  def test_double_injection_raises(self):
+    m = _tiny_model()
+    peft.inject_diagscale(m, pattern=".")
+    with self.assertRaises(ValueError):
+      peft.inject_diagscale(m, pattern=".")
+
+  def test_ia3_identity_and_selection(self):
+    m = _tiny_model()
+    x, y, train_size = _dummy_batch()
+    with torch.no_grad():
+      before = m(x, y, train_size)
+    selected = peft.select_trainable(m, mode="ia3", pattern=".", parts=("k", "v", "ff"))
+    with torch.no_grad():
+      after = m(x, y, train_size)
+    torch.testing.assert_close(before, after, rtol=0, atol=0)
+    n_attn = sum(1 for _, mod in m.named_modules() if isinstance(mod, MultiheadAttention))
+    # k + v per attention, ff per block (blocks == attentions in this model: every
+    # MultiheadAttentionBlock owns exactly one MultiheadAttention).
+    self.assertEqual(len(selected), 3 * n_attn)
+    for name, p in m.named_parameters():
+      is_ia3 = name.rsplit(".", 1)[-1] in ("ia3_delta_k", "ia3_delta_v", "ia3_delta_ff")
+      self.assertEqual(p.requires_grad, is_ia3, name)
+
+  def test_ia3_parts_subset_and_gradients(self):
+    m = _tiny_model()
+    selected = peft.select_trainable(m, mode="ia3", pattern=".", parts=("k", "v"))
+    names = [n for n, p in m.named_parameters() if p.requires_grad]
+    self.assertTrue(all(n.endswith((".ia3_delta_k", ".ia3_delta_v")) for n in names))
+    self.assertFalse(any(n.endswith(".ia3_delta_ff") for n in names))
+    x, y, train_size = _dummy_batch()
+    out = m(x, y, train_size)
+    out.square().mean().backward()
+    self.assertTrue(any(p.grad is not None and p.grad.abs().sum() > 0 for p in selected))
+
+  def test_state_dict_roundtrip_before_injection(self):
+    """Checkpoint loading (strict) must be unaffected when nothing is injected."""
+    m1, m2 = _tiny_model(seed=1), _tiny_model(seed=2)
+    m2.load_state_dict(m1.state_dict(), strict=True)
+    x, y, train_size = _dummy_batch()
+    with torch.no_grad():
+      torch.testing.assert_close(m1(x, y, train_size), m2(x, y, train_size))
+
+
+if __name__ == "__main__":
+  unittest.main()

+"""Parameter-selection hooks for PEFT-style finetuning of TabICL
+(ttc diagscale fork addition, mirroring the tabfm fork's pytorch/peft.py).
+
+select_trainable() decides which parameters a finetuning optimizer sees;
+everything else is frozen (requires_grad=False).
+
+Modes:
+    full       all parameters, requires_grad untouched (plain full finetuning)
+    regex      parameters whose fully-qualified name matches cfg["pattern"]
+    diagscale  inject a fresh per-(head, dim) multiplier 1+delta on the query of
+               every MultiheadAttention whose MODULE name matches
+               cfg["pattern"], then train ONLY the deltas. delta is
+               zero-initialized so injection is behavior-preserving (the model
+               reproduces the pretrained model exactly until training moves
+               delta). The multiplier is applied to the final query right
+               before SDPA — after RoPE and any SSMax scaling — so the
+               attention logit is exactly a learned diagonal reweighting of
+               the similarity bilinear form: q̃·k = qᵀ diag(1+δ) k.
+    ia3        (IA)3 (Liu et al. 2022) baseline: 1+delta multipliers on the
+               final keys / values and/or the FFN intermediate activation,
+               cfg["parts"] ⊆ {"k","v","ff"} (default all three). Same
+               zero-init delta convention as diagscale so lr/wd sweeps are
+               comparable; the k part is logit-equivalent to diagscale's
+               q-side delta.
+
+Useful patterns (module names of the released TabICL v2 checkpoints):
+    "."                    all 21 attention modules
+    r"^col_embedder\\."     the column embedder's set transformer (6 modules) —
+                           the most upstream attention that reads train rows
+    r"^icl_predictor\\."    the 12 ICL blocks attending over train rows
+"""
+
+from __future__ import annotations
+
+import logging
+import re
+from typing import Any
+
+import torch
+
+from .layers import MultiheadAttention, MultiheadAttentionBlock
+
+logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
+
+
+def inject_diagscale(model: torch.nn.Module, pattern: str) -> int:
+    """Register a zero-init diag_delta (num_heads, 1, head_dim) on every
+    MultiheadAttention whose module name matches ``pattern``. In-place;
+    returns the number of modules injected.
+
+    delta is created float32 regardless of the backbone dtype (the forward
+    casts 1+delta to the compute dtype), so tiny optimizer updates are not
+    lost to low-precision rounding. No RNG needed (deterministic zero-init).
+    """
+    rx = re.compile(pattern)
+    targets = [(n, m) for n, m in model.named_modules() if isinstance(m, MultiheadAttention) and rx.search(n)]
+    for name, mod in targets:
+        if mod.diag_delta is not None:
+            raise ValueError(f"diagscale already injected at {name!r}")
+        mod.diag_delta = torch.nn.Parameter(
+            torch.zeros(mod.num_heads, 1, mod.head_dim, device=mod.in_proj_weight.device, dtype=torch.float32)
+        )
+    return len(targets)
+
+
+def inject_ia3(model: torch.nn.Module, pattern: str, parts: tuple = ("k", "v", "ff")) -> int:
+    """(IA)3 (Liu et al. 2022) adapters as zero-init deltas: 1+delta multipliers
+    on the final keys / values ((num_heads, 1, head_dim)) and/or the FFN
+    intermediate activation ((dim_feedforward,)). ``pattern`` matches module
+    names; ``parts`` selects which of k / v / ff to inject. Returns the number
+    of injected tensors.
+
+    Zero-init deltas (rather than the paper's ones-init vectors) keep the
+    multiplier identity at start AND give AdamW weight decay the correct prior
+    (pull toward the pretrained model, not toward a zero function) —
+    consistent with diagscale, so lr/wd sweeps are directly comparable across
+    the two adapter families.
+    """
+    rx = re.compile(pattern)
+    n = 0
+    if "k" in parts or "v" in parts:
+        for name, mod in model.named_modules():
+            if isinstance(mod, MultiheadAttention) and rx.search(name):
+                shape = (mod.num_heads, 1, mod.head_dim)
+                device = mod.in_proj_weight.device
+                if "k" in parts:
+                    if mod.ia3_delta_k is not None:
+                        raise ValueError(f"ia3 (k) already injected at {name!r}")
+                    mod.ia3_delta_k = torch.nn.Parameter(torch.zeros(*shape, device=device, dtype=torch.float32))
+                    n += 1
+                if "v" in parts:
+                    if mod.ia3_delta_v is not None:
+                        raise ValueError(f"ia3 (v) already injected at {name!r}")
+                    mod.ia3_delta_v = torch.nn.Parameter(torch.zeros(*shape, device=device, dtype=torch.float32))
+                    n += 1
+    if "ff" in parts:
+        for name, mod in model.named_modules():
+            if isinstance(mod, MultiheadAttentionBlock) and rx.search(name):
+                if mod.ia3_delta_ff is not None:
+                    raise ValueError(f"ia3 (ff) already injected at {name!r}")
+                mod.ia3_delta_ff = torch.nn.Parameter(
+                    torch.zeros(mod.linear2.in_features, device=mod.linear2.weight.device, dtype=torch.float32)
+                )
+                n += 1
+    return n
+
+
+def select_trainable(
+    model: torch.nn.Module,
+    mode: str = "full",
+    freeze_others: bool = True,
+    **cfg: Any,
+) -> list[torch.nn.Parameter]:
+    """Freeze/unfreeze ``model``'s parameters per ``mode``; return the trainables.
+
+    ``freeze_others=False`` (adapter modes only) selects the same trainable set but
+    leaves every backbone parameter's requires_grad flag untouched. On CUDA, flipping
+    requires_grad on the col_embedder's parameters deterministically shifts the
+    train-mode forward by ~5e-3 even at delta=0 (kernel selection keyed on the flag
+    somewhere in that path; CPU is bit-exact, exact op not pinned down) — a caller
+    that trains only the returned parameters anyway can keep flag parity with the
+    unfrozen model and stay bit-exact at injection.
+    """
+    named = list(model.named_parameters())
+    n_total = sum(p.numel() for _, p in named)
+
+    if mode == "full":
+        # requires_grad untouched; skip params that are non-trainable by design
+        # (each RotaryEmbedding's freqs when learned_freq=False)
+        selected = [p for _, p in named if p.requires_grad]
+    elif mode == "diagscale":
+        n_injected = inject_diagscale(model, pattern=cfg["pattern"])
+        if n_injected == 0:
+            raise ValueError(f"finetune_mode='diagscale' (cfg={cfg}) matched 0 MultiheadAttention modules")
+        selected = []
+        for name, param in model.named_parameters():  # re-enumerate: deltas now exist
+            train = name.endswith(".diag_delta")
+            if freeze_others:
+                param.requires_grad_(train)
+            if train:
+                selected.append(param)
+        if not selected:
+            raise ValueError(f"finetune_mode='diagscale' injected {n_injected} module(s) but selected 0 params")
+        logger.info("finetune_mode=diagscale: injected %d attention module(s)", n_injected)
+    elif mode == "ia3":
+        parts = tuple(cfg.get("parts", ("k", "v", "ff")))
+        n_injected = inject_ia3(model, pattern=cfg.get("pattern", "."), parts=parts)
+        if n_injected == 0:
+            raise ValueError(f"finetune_mode='ia3' (cfg={cfg}) matched 0 modules")
+        selected = []
+        for name, param in model.named_parameters():  # re-enumerate: deltas now exist
+            train = any(name.endswith(f".ia3_delta_{p}") for p in ("k", "v", "ff"))
+            if freeze_others:
+                param.requires_grad_(train)
+            if train:
+                selected.append(param)
+        if not selected:
+            raise ValueError(f"finetune_mode='ia3' injected {n_injected} tensor(s) but selected 0 params")
+        logger.info("finetune_mode=ia3: injected %d tensor(s) (parts=%s)", n_injected, parts)
+    elif mode == "regex":
+        if not freeze_others:
+            raise ValueError("freeze_others=False is only supported for adapter modes (diagscale/ia3)")
+        rx = re.compile(cfg["pattern"])
+        selected = []
+        for name, param in named:
+            train = rx.search(name) is not None
+            param.requires_grad_(train)
+            if train:
+                selected.append(param)
+        if not selected:
+            raise ValueError(f"finetune_mode='regex' (cfg={cfg}) selected 0 parameters")
+    else:
+        raise ValueError(f"unknown finetune_mode {mode!r} (full | regex | diagscale | ia3)")
+
+    n_selected = sum(p.numel() for p in selected)
+    msg = (
+        f"finetune_mode={mode}: trainable {n_selected:,} / {n_total:,} params "
+        f"({100 * n_selected / n_total:.4f}%)"
+    )
+    logger.info(msg)
+    print(f"[tabicl.peft] {msg}", flush=True)  # visibility in job logs
+    return selected

+++ b/tests/test_peft.py
@@ -0,0 +1,187 @@
+"""Tests for the diagscale / (IA)3 PEFT adapters (ttc fork addition).
+
+The load-bearing property is identity-at-injection: a zero-init delta must
+leave the pretrained model's outputs bit-exactly unchanged, on the same
+forward path the finetuner uses (including qassmax ssmax layers).
+"""
+
+import pytest
+import torch
+
+from tabicl._model.tabicl import TabICL
+from tabicl._model.layers import MultiheadAttention, MultiheadAttentionBlock
+from tabicl._model.peft import inject_diagscale, inject_ia3, select_trainable
+
+
+def make_model(**kwargs) -> TabICL:
+    torch.manual_seed(0)
+    defaults = dict(
+        max_classes=4,
+        embed_dim=32,
+        col_num_blocks=1,
+        col_nhead=2,
+        col_num_inds=8,
+        row_num_blocks=1,
+        row_nhead=2,
+        icl_num_blocks=2,
+        icl_nhead=2,
+        # random (not zeroed) out_proj/linear2 so adapters influence outputs
+        zero_init=False,
+    )
+    defaults.update(kwargs)
+    return TabICL(**defaults).eval()
+
+
+def make_batch(n_rows=24, n_features=5, train_size=16, n_classes=3):
+    g = torch.Generator().manual_seed(1)
+    X = torch.randn(1, n_rows, n_features, generator=g)
+    y_train = torch.randint(0, n_classes, (1, train_size), generator=g).float()
+    return X, y_train
+
+
+def forward(model, X, y_train):
+    with torch.no_grad():
+        return model(X, y_train)
+
+
+def n_attention_modules(model):
+    return sum(1 for _, m in model.named_modules() if isinstance(m, MultiheadAttention))
+
+
+def test_diagscale_identity_at_init():
+    model = make_model()
+    X, y_train = make_batch()
+    before = forward(model, X, y_train)
+    n = inject_diagscale(model, ".")
+    assert n == n_attention_modules(model)
+    after = forward(model, X, y_train)
+    assert torch.equal(before, after)
+
+
+def test_ia3_identity_at_init():
+    model = make_model()
+    X, y_train = make_batch()
+    before = forward(model, X, y_train)
+    inject_ia3(model, ".", parts=("k", "v", "ff"))
+    after = forward(model, X, y_train)
+    assert torch.equal(before, after)
+
+
+def test_nonzero_delta_changes_output():
+    model = make_model()
+    X, y_train = make_batch()
+    before = forward(model, X, y_train)
+    inject_diagscale(model, ".")
+    with torch.no_grad():
+        for _, m in model.named_modules():
+            if isinstance(m, MultiheadAttention):
+                m.diag_delta.fill_(0.3)
+    after = forward(model, X, y_train)
+    assert not torch.allclose(before, after)
+
+
+def test_injection_counts_and_scopes():
+    model = make_model()
+    # col: 2 attentions per ISAB block, row: 1 per block, icl: 1 per block
+    assert n_attention_modules(model) == 2 * 1 + 1 + 2
+    assert inject_diagscale(model, r"^icl_predictor\.") == 2
+    assert inject_diagscale(model, r"^col_embedder\.") == 2
+    assert inject_diagscale(model, r"^row_interactor\.") == 1
+
+
+def test_double_injection_raises():
+    model = make_model()
+    inject_diagscale(model, r"^icl_predictor\.")
+    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="already injected"):
+        inject_diagscale(model, ".")
+
+
+def test_select_trainable_diagscale():
+    model = make_model()
+    selected = select_trainable(model, mode="diagscale", pattern=".")
+    expected = sum(m.embed_dim for _, m in model.named_modules() if isinstance(m, MultiheadAttention))
+    assert sum(p.numel() for p in selected) == expected
+    for name, p in model.named_parameters():
+        assert p.requires_grad == name.endswith(".diag_delta")
+
+
+def test_select_trainable_ia3_parts():
+    model = make_model()
+    n_attn = n_attention_modules(model)
+    n_blocks = sum(1 for _, m in model.named_modules() if isinstance(m, MultiheadAttentionBlock))
+    n = inject_ia3(model, ".", parts=("k", "ff"))
+    assert n == n_attn + n_blocks
+    ks = [p for name, p in model.named_parameters() if name.endswith(".ia3_delta_k")]
+    vs = [p for name, p in model.named_parameters() if name.endswith(".ia3_delta_v")]
+    ffs = [p for name, p in model.named_parameters() if name.endswith(".ia3_delta_ff")]
+    assert len(ks) == n_attn and len(vs) == 0 and len(ffs) == n_blocks
+
+
+def test_gradient_flow_to_delta_only():
+    model = make_model()
+    select_trainable(model, mode="diagscale", pattern=".")
+    model.train()
+    X, y_train = make_batch()
+    out = model(X, y_train)
+    out.sum().backward()
+    grads = [p.grad for name, p in model.named_parameters() if name.endswith(".diag_delta")]
+    assert all(g is not None for g in grads)
+    assert any(g.abs().sum() > 0 for g in grads)
+    frozen = [p.grad for name, p in model.named_parameters() if not name.endswith(".diag_delta")]
+    assert all(g is None for g in frozen)
+
+
+def test_select_trainable_no_freeze():
+    model = make_model()
+    flags_before = {n: p.requires_grad for n, p in model.named_parameters()}
+    selected = select_trainable(model, mode="diagscale", pattern=".", freeze_others=False)
+    expected = sum(m.embed_dim for _, m in model.named_modules() if isinstance(m, MultiheadAttention))
+    assert sum(p.numel() for p in selected) == expected
+    # backbone flags untouched (kernel parity with the unfrozen model on CUDA);
+    # note rope.freqs is requires_grad=False by construction, so compare, not all-True
+    for n, p in model.named_parameters():
+        assert p.requires_grad == flags_before.get(n, True), n
+
+
+def test_ia3_cached_kv_matches_fresh_path():
+    # the KV cache must store PRE-rescale projections: with nonzero ia3 deltas the
+    # cached path must reproduce the fresh path exactly once (not (1+delta)^2)
+    from tabicl._model.kv_cache import KVCacheEntry
+
+    model = make_model()
+    inject_ia3(model, ".", parts=("k", "v"))
+    with torch.no_grad():
+        for _, m in model.named_modules():
+            if isinstance(m, MultiheadAttention):
+                m.ia3_delta_k.fill_(0.3)
+                m.ia3_delta_v.fill_(-0.2)
+    mha = next(m for _, m in model.named_modules() if isinstance(m, MultiheadAttention))
+    g = torch.Generator().manual_seed(2)
+    q = torch.randn(1, 6, mha.embed_dim, generator=g)
+    kv = torch.randn(1, 10, mha.embed_dim, generator=g)
+    with torch.no_grad():
+        fresh, k_cache, v_cache = mha(q, kv, kv, need_kv=True)
+        cached = mha(q, cached_kv=KVCacheEntry(key=k_cache, value=v_cache))
+    assert torch.allclose(fresh, cached, atol=1e-6)
+
+
+def test_state_dict_roundtrip():
+    model_a = make_model()
+    inject_diagscale(model_a, ".")
+    with torch.no_grad():
+        for _, m in model_a.named_modules():
+            if isinstance(m, MultiheadAttention):
+                m.diag_delta.fill_(0.1)
+    model_b = make_model()
+    inject_diagscale(model_b, ".")
+    model_b.load_state_dict(model_a.state_dict(), strict=True)
+    X, y_train = make_batch()
+    assert torch.equal(forward(model_a, X, y_train), forward(model_b, X, y_train))
+
+
+def test_pretrained_state_dict_loads_without_adapters():
+    # adapter slots must not appear in state_dict before injection, so
+    # released checkpoints keep loading with strict=True
+    model = make_model()
+    assert not any("delta" in k for k in model.state_dict())
+    model.load_state_dict(make_model().state_dict(), strict=True)

+"""Parameter-selection hook for PEFT-style finetuning (ttc-dev fork addition).
+
+select_trainable() decides which parameters the finetuning optimizer sees; everything else is
+frozen (requires_grad=False). Wired into FinetunedTabPFNBase via the `finetune_mode` /
+`finetune_cfg` constructor arguments.
+
+Modes:
+    full    all parameters, requires_grad untouched (the original behavior — must stay
+            equivalent to upstream; guarded by the validation ladder's identity rungs)
+    bitfit  bias terms only (BitFit, Ben Zaken et al. 2022)
+    regex   parameters whose fully-qualified name matches finetune_cfg["pattern"] — the
+            explicit form of "train only these layers"; inspect names first via
+            `[n for n, _ in estimator.model_.named_parameters()]`
+    lora    inject low-rank adapters (Hu et al. 2021) into the nn.Linear MODULES whose name
+            matches finetune_cfg["pattern"], freeze the base weights, train only the adapters.
+            finetune_cfg: {pattern, r=8, alpha=16, dropout=0.0}. NB pattern matches MODULE names
+            (e.g. "...attention.q_proj"), not parameter names — inspect via
+            `[n for n, m in estimator.model_.named_modules() if isinstance(m, torch.nn.Linear)]`.
+"""
+from __future__ import annotations
+
+import logging
+import math
+import re
+from typing import Any
+
+import torch
+import torch.nn.functional as F
+
+logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
+
+
+class LoRALinear(torch.nn.Module):
+    """Wrap an nn.Linear with a frozen base + a trainable low-rank update B@A·(alpha/r).
+
+    B is zero-initialized so the adapter starts as a no-op (forward == base): injection is
+    therefore behavior-preserving, which also means a never-improved fit (best_epoch=-1) restored
+    to these init weights reproduces base exactly — consistent with the base fall-back.
+    """
+
+    def __init__(self, base: torch.nn.Linear, r: int, alpha: float, dropout: float):
+        super().__init__()
+        if int(r) <= 0:
+            raise ValueError(f"LoRA rank must be a positive int, got r={r!r}")
+        self.base = base
+        self.base.weight.requires_grad_(False)
+        if self.base.bias is not None:
+            self.base.bias.requires_grad_(False)
+        self.r = int(r)
+        self.scaling = float(alpha) / float(r)
+        w = base.weight
+        self.lora_A = torch.nn.Parameter(torch.empty(self.r, base.in_features, device=w.device, dtype=w.dtype))
+        self.lora_B = torch.nn.Parameter(torch.zeros(base.out_features, self.r, device=w.device, dtype=w.dtype))
+        torch.nn.init.kaiming_uniform_(self.lora_A, a=math.sqrt(5))
+        self.lora_dropout = torch.nn.Dropout(dropout) if dropout and dropout > 0 else torch.nn.Identity()
+
+    def forward(self, x: torch.Tensor) -> torch.Tensor:  # base(x) + (x A^T) B^T · scaling
+        return self.base(x) + F.linear(F.linear(self.lora_dropout(x), self.lora_A), self.lora_B) * self.scaling
+
+
+def inject_lora(
+    model: torch.nn.Module, pattern: str, r: int, alpha: float, dropout: float, seed: int = 0
+) -> int:
+    """Replace every nn.Linear whose module name matches `pattern` with a LoRALinear. In-place;
+    returns the number of layers wrapped.
+
+    The adapter init (kaiming on lora_A) is made reproducible by seeding the RNG to `seed` for the
+    duration of injection, inside a fork_rng so the global RNG the training loop later relies on is
+    left untouched. Without this, lora_A would draw from the un-seeded global RNG (injection runs
+    before infer_random_state), so identical random_state gave different adapters across runs.
+    """
+    rx = re.compile(pattern)
+    targets = [
+        (n, m) for n, m in model.named_modules()
+        if isinstance(m, torch.nn.Linear) and rx.search(n)
+    ]
+    cuda_idx = sorted({p.device.index for p in model.parameters()
+                       if p.is_cuda and p.device.index is not None})
+    with torch.random.fork_rng(devices=cuda_idx, enabled=True):
+        torch.manual_seed(int(seed))
+        for name, lin in targets:
+            parent_path, _, attr = name.rpartition(".")
+            parent = model.get_submodule(parent_path) if parent_path else model
+            setattr(parent, attr, LoRALinear(lin, r=r, alpha=alpha, dropout=dropout))
+    return len(targets)
+
+
+class DiagScale(torch.nn.Module):
+    """Wrap a SoftmaxScalingMLP with a frozen base + a trainable per-(head,dim) multiplier 1+delta.
+
+    delta is zero-initialized so injection starts as a no-op (forward == base). This is the direct,
+    minimal parameterization of the "diagonal similarity metric" hypothesis: rather than finetuning
+    base_mlp, learn a bare constant scale on the query, q̃ = base(q,n) * (1+delta). delta [H,D] is then
+    literally the learned metric correction (readable without reconstructing s from base_mlp weights).
+    Mirrors LoRALinear: frozen base, zero-init adapter, wrapped in place at select_trainable time
+    (after the checkpoint load), so it never interferes with strict loading.
+    """
+
+    def __init__(self, base: torch.nn.Module):
+        super().__init__()
+        self.base = base
+        for p in self.base.parameters():
+            p.requires_grad_(False)
+        h, d = int(base.num_heads), int(base.head_dim)
+        ref = next(self.base.parameters())
+        self.delta = torch.nn.Parameter(torch.zeros(h, d, device=ref.device, dtype=ref.dtype))
+
+    def forward(self, q: torch.Tensor, n: int) -> torch.Tensor:  # base(q,n) * (1+delta), [B,S,H,D]*[H,D]
+        return self.base(q, n) * (1.0 + self.delta)
+
+
+def inject_diagscale(model: torch.nn.Module, pattern: str) -> int:
+    """Replace every SoftmaxScalingMLP whose MODULE name matches `pattern` with a DiagScale wrapper.
+    In-place; returns the number wrapped. No RNG needed (delta is deterministic zero-init)."""
+    rx = re.compile(pattern)
+    targets = [(n, m) for n, m in model.named_modules()
+               if type(m).__name__ == "SoftmaxScalingMLP" and rx.search(n)]
+    for name, mod in targets:
+        parent_path, _, attr = name.rpartition(".")
+        parent = model.get_submodule(parent_path) if parent_path else model
+        setattr(parent, attr, DiagScale(mod))
+    return len(targets)
+
+
+def select_trainable(
+    model: torch.nn.Module,
+    mode: str = "full",
+    **cfg: Any,
+) -> list[torch.nn.Parameter]:
+    """Freeze/unfreeze `model`'s parameters per `mode`; return the trainable parameters."""
+    named = list(model.named_parameters())
+    n_total = sum(p.numel() for _, p in named)
+
+    if mode == "full":
+        selected = [p for _, p in named]  # requires_grad untouched: exact upstream behavior
+    elif mode == "lora":
+        n_wrapped = inject_lora(
+            model,
+            pattern=cfg["pattern"],
+            r=int(cfg.get("r", 8)),
+            alpha=float(cfg.get("alpha", 16)),
+            dropout=float(cfg.get("dropout", 0.0)),
+            seed=int(cfg.get("seed", 0)),
+        )
+        if n_wrapped == 0:
+            raise ValueError(f"finetune_mode='lora' (cfg={cfg}) matched 0 nn.Linear modules")
+        selected = []
+        for name, param in model.named_parameters():  # re-enumerate: adapters now exist
+            train = name.endswith(".lora_A") or name.endswith(".lora_B")
+            param.requires_grad_(train)
+            if train:
+                selected.append(param)
+        if not selected:  # wrapped Linears but no adapter params — should never happen, guard anyway
+            raise ValueError(f"finetune_mode='lora' wrapped {n_wrapped} module(s) but selected 0 params")
+        logger.info("finetune_mode=lora: wrapped %d Linear module(s)", n_wrapped)
+    elif mode == "diagscale":
+        n_wrapped = inject_diagscale(model, pattern=cfg["pattern"])
+        if n_wrapped == 0:
+            raise ValueError(f"finetune_mode='diagscale' (cfg={cfg}) matched 0 SoftmaxScalingMLP modules")
+        selected = []
+        for name, param in model.named_parameters():  # re-enumerate: delta params now exist
+            train = name.endswith(".delta")
+            param.requires_grad_(train)
+            if train:
+                selected.append(param)
+        if not selected:
+            raise ValueError(f"finetune_mode='diagscale' wrapped {n_wrapped} module(s) but selected 0 params")
+        logger.info("finetune_mode=diagscale: wrapped %d SoftmaxScalingMLP module(s)", n_wrapped)
+    else:
+        if mode == "bitfit":
+            def keep(name: str) -> bool:
+                return name.endswith(".bias")
+        elif mode == "regex":
+            pattern = re.compile(cfg["pattern"])
+
+            def keep(name: str) -> bool:
+                return pattern.search(name) is not None
+        else:
+            raise ValueError(f"unknown finetune_mode '{mode}' (full | bitfit | regex | lora | diagscale)")
+        selected = []
+        for name, param in named:
+            train = keep(name)
+            param.requires_grad_(train)
+            if train:
+                selected.append(param)
+        if not selected:
+            raise ValueError(f"finetune_mode='{mode}' (cfg={cfg}) selected 0 parameters")
+
+    n_selected = sum(p.numel() for p in selected)
+    msg = (
+        f"finetune_mode={mode}: trainable {n_selected:,} / {n_total:,} params "
+        f"({100 * n_selected / n_total:.2f}%)"
+    )
+    logger.info(msg)
+    print(f"[tabpfn.finetuning.peft] {msg}", flush=True)  # ensure visibility in job logs
+    return selected



================================================

Subject: [PATCH 1/3] feat: run inference in the model's parameter dtype (bf16

MIME-Version: 1.0
Content-Type: text/plain; charset=UTF-8
Content-Transfer-Encoding: 8bit

Two changes to the PyTorch predict path so a bf16-cast backbone runs
end-to-end (halves activation memory; lets wide datasets like APSFailure
76k x 170 fit a single 80GB GPU at full context):

- _predict_step_pytorch: cast the input batch to the model's parameter
  dtype instead of hardcoding float32 (backward-compatible: an fp32
  model still gets fp32 inputs)
- return out_t.float().cpu().numpy() — numpy has no bfloat16, so cast
  logits back to fp32 before leaving torch
---

   device = next(model.parameters()).device
 
-  X_t = torch.from_numpy(X_batch).to(device, dtype=torch.float32)
+  # Match the model's parameter dtype instead of hard-coding fp32, so a bf16-cast backbone runs
+  # end-to-end in bf16 (halves activation memory -> large/WIDE datasets fit a single GPU at full
+  # context). Backward-compatible: an fp32 model still gets fp32 inputs. [ttc: TabFMModel bf16]
+  X_t = torch.from_numpy(X_batch).to(device, dtype=next(model.parameters()).dtype)

Port of the TabPFN-3 diagscale PEFT recipe (learned diagonal similarity
metric) to the PyTorch TabFM. inject_diagscale registers a zero-init
delta [nhead, hd] on matched MultiheadAttention modules; the forward
multiplies the FINAL query (after RoPE / q-RMSNorm / per_dim_scale,
before SDPA-at-scale-1.0) by 1+delta, so q̃·k = qᵀ diag(1+δ) k exactly
and injection is behavior-preserving at init. select_trainable(mode=
full|regex|diagscale) picks the optimizer's params, mirroring the
tabpfn fork's finetuning/peft.py.

     self.per_dim_scale = nn.Parameter(torch.zeros(self.hd))
+    # Optional diagscale PEFT adapter (see peft.py): a per-(head,dim) query
+    # multiplier 1+delta, zero-init at injection time so it starts as a no-op.
+    # Plain None here (not a Parameter) so checkpoints load strict=True; the

     scale = 1.442695041 / math.sqrt(self.hd) * F.softplus(self.per_dim_scale.float())
     q = q * scale.to(q.dtype)
+    if self.diag_delta is not None:

     # bf16 SDPA (flash already does the softmax in float32 internally).
